OIL FUTURES: US crude pushes to one-month high amid Middle East supply concerns
Four vessels hit near Hormuz since Oct. 16
WTI reaches its highest level since Sept. 15
Trump waives sanctions on Russian oil buyers
US crude futures settled at one-month highs Oct. 19, as continued attacks on shipping pressured Middle East supply outlooks.
NYMEX November WTI settled $2.25/barrel higher at $103.66/b, while ICE December Brent declined 31 cents/b to end the session at $109.55/b.
Four ship attacks have been reported since Oct. 16, including a rocket-propelled grenade attack on a tanker near Jubail, Saudi Arabia, in the Gulf region, according to UK Maritime Trade Operations.
The latest attack occurred Oct. 18, when a tugboat traveling southbound northeast of Khor Fakkan, UAE, outside the Strait of Hormuz, was struck by two unknown projectiles, resulting in damage and a "minor casualty," UKMTO said on its website. The tug subsequently continued its journey, according to UKMTO.
"There are strong indications that oil exports from the Persian Gulf are once again under pressure following a sharp increase in reported attacks on shipping in and around the Strait of Hormuz in October," Arne Lohmann Rasmussen, chief analyst at Global Risk Management, said.
Front-month NYMEX WTI last settled higher on Sept. 15. Strait of Hormuz transits climbed to 23 ships on Oct. 18, with more product tankers and bulk carriers taking the chokepoint

International Crude
CHINA DATA: Sep crude throughput hits 6-month high of 14 mil b/d
Sep domestic crude output at 4.45 mil b/d
Apparent stock draw reaches 531,000 b/d in Sep
Q3 GDP expands 4.5% year over year
China's crude throughput recovered to a six-month high of 14.04 million barrels/day (57.48 million metric tons) in September, according to data from the National Bureau of Statistics on Oct. 19.
Despite an 8.4% year-over-year reduction among the same sample of refineries monitored by NBS, the volume crossed the 14-million-b/d mark for the first time since reaching 14.58 million b/d in March, the data showed.
In September, combined crude throughput at China's 49 state-owned refineries rose for a fourth consecutive month to a six-month high of 8.39 million b/d, Platts data showed. Based on a combined refining capacity of 11 million b/d, utilization reached 76.4%, marking a third consecutive monthly increase since June.
A month-over-month increase in the country's throughput, on a barrels-per-day basis, remained despite being partly offset by private refiners cutting throughput since mid-September amid higher feedstock costs, even as they recently received about 60.57 million mt of flash crude import quotas.
S&P Global Energy CERA estimated that state-run refineries' refining margins rose 5 cents/b month over month to $22.44/b, while independent refiners' margins eased by $1.62/b to $4.2/b, according to its monthly report dated Sept. 30.
On a barrels-per-day basis, the average throughput in September represented 0.8% growth from August. But on a metric-tons basis, the volume fell 2.7% month over month from 59.07 million mt, NBS data showed.

Stock draw The increase in throughput outpaced the rise in supply,
leading to an apparent stock draw of about 531,000 b/d in September, Platts calculations based on throughput, output and import data showed.
The higher daily throughput came as China's domestic crude output also hit a six-month high of 4.45 million b/d (18.21 million mt) in September, NBS data showed, while imports rose to a five-month high of 9.06 million b/d, according to customs data released Oct. 14.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 19, 2026

China's crude stocks fell to 1.252 billion barrels at the end of September from 1.307 billion barrels at the end of August and a record high of 1.382 billion barrels at the end of May, according to satellite data from Ursa Space.
In the first three quarters of 2026, China's crude throughput averaged 13.79 million b/d, down 4.8% year over year, while the country's GDP expanded 4.6% over the period, NBS data showed.
In the third quarter, China's economy grew 4.5% year over year, up from 4.3% in the second quarter, according to NBS. GDP growth in the second quarter was the lowest since 2.9% in Q4 2022.
Looking at October, China's refining throughput is likely to retreat slightly from September as independent refineries have been cutting runs amid high feedstock costs and limited supplies.
Meanwhile, state-run refineries are expected to boost throughput in the second half of October due to rising deliveries from Saudi Arabia, following a reduction in the first half from September, two refining sources said Oct. 19.
CERA anticipates that China's total refinery throughput will decline by 1.2 million b/d in 2026 year over year, according to its monthly report.

December loadings of Angolan crudes provisionally set to average 993,065 b/d
Total loadings set to rise by 4.435 million barrels
Monthly loadings set to increase by five cargoes
Angola is set to load 32 cargoes of crude oil in December, according to a copy of the producer's provisional loading schedule seen by Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, on Oct. 19.
Total loading volumes have been set at 30.785 million barrels across 32 cargoes in December, up 4.435 million barrels from the finalized November loading program.
With five more cargoes provisionally set to load in December compared with November, December volumes are set to average 993,065 barrels/day -- an increase of 114,731 b/d from November's average of 878,333 b/d.
ExxonMobil, Equinor, Chevron and Sonangol have been named among the lifters in the provisional program. Sonangol, Angola's state oil company, is listed as the lifter of 7.65 million barrels, representing just under 25% of total barrels set for loading.

CRUDE MOC: Platts Dubai differential slides below $4/b premium
Cash Dubai, Oman at premiums of $3.83/b
31 Dubai partials trade during MOC
Cash Dubai flips to discount to cash Brent
Cash differentials for Middle East medium sour crude markers weakened for the seventh straight session during the Singapore Platts Market on Close assessment process Oct. 19, with premiums sliding below $4/b.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed December cash Dubai and cash Oman at premiums of $3.83/barrel over samemonth Dubai futures at market close, down $1/b from the previous session.
December cash Murban fell $1.40/b during the session to a premium of $6/b over same-month Dubai futures.
During the MOC, 31 December Dubai partials, each of 25,000 barrels, were traded. The sellers were BP, Mitsui, Unipec and Glencore, while the buyers were Trafigura and ExxonMobil.
No convergences were reached during the MOC. A convergence occurs when 20 partials are traded between two counterparties in the same direction, resulting in a full 500,000-barrel physical cargo being declared from the seller to the buyer.
Front-month cash Dubai flipped to a discount to cash Brent for the first time since mid-August. Platts assessed the

Turkey targets 1 million b/d crude output from state-operator TPAO
Further expansion of Gabar and Adiyaman fields underway Exploration continuing in Black Sea, more drilling in Somalia TPAO targeting possible acquisitions in North America, Africa
Turkey wants to expand state upstream operator TPAO's long-term crude oil portfolio to 1 million barrels oil equivalent per day, its energy minister Alparslan Bayraktar said in a series of TV interviews October 17-18 after the dispatch of the company's floating production platform to its Black Sea Sakarya gas field.

Bayraktar said that Ankara plans to meet the 1 million boe/d target through a combination of expanding domestic crude production operations, growth outside Turkey in countries where the company holds stakes in exploration licenses, and acquisitions.
The minister estimated the company's existing production at roughly 350,000 boe/d and set an interim production target of 550,000-600,000 boe/d by 2028.
In Turkey, TPAO is producing 84,000 b/d from its south eastern Gabar fields, Bayraktar said, with the potential to rise to 100,000 b/d. The company is also close to disclosing a new reserve to the west of Gabar, but is not yet ready to annouce its estimated reserves, he said.
In addition, the state-backed operator has been working to expand production at its Adiyaman fields, also in southeast Turkey, where production has fallen under 10,000 b/d. New discoveries are expected to boost output to round 15,000 b/d he said.
Bayraktar also said that TPAO is still prospecting in the Black Sea, with work underway on its Akcabeyli well, in the west Black Sea.
"This time we are drilling a well with indications of oil," he said, confirming that TPAO plans to extend drilling to 6,000 meters below sea level.
Overseas operations Outside of Turkey, Bayraktar said that TPAO may drill a

Platts North Sea Crude Daily Market Analysis
Volatility persists amid freight uncertainty
WTI Midland differentials weaken in Europe
The North Sea crude oil market extended its declines Oct. 19 amid a volatile pricing environment.
Freight costs were heard to have stabilized at a lower level during the session, following a selloff through the previous week.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the US Gulf Coast-UK/continent Aframax freight rate at $136.57/metric ton Oct. 16, down $27.31/mt day over day and down $61.97/mt week over week.
"The headlines are that [the Trump Administration] is going to lift some sanctions on vessels, but it seems that's not easy to take effect [immediately]," a North Sea crude oil trader said Oct. 19.
In a video address to the World Petroleum Congress last week, US Energy Secretary Chris Wright acknowledged the possibility of easing sanctions on shadow-fleet tankers linked to Russia, Venezuela and Iran in a US bid to combat soaring freight costs.
Landed differentials of WTI Midland in Europe were again demonstrated lower during the Platts Market on Close assessment process Oct. 19.
TotalEnergies, BP, and Occidental offered WTI Midland cargoes during the session and showed weaker premiums for November arrival dates. The orders, at premiums of between $10/

barrel and $12/b over Dated Brent, were left outstanding at the London close.
Second-half November arriving volumes were heard pegged at a premium of $9/b to $10/b over November Dated Brent, according to the Europe-based crude trader, slightly higher than values heard in the previous session.
"The market is a bit volatile," a second North Sea crude trader said, noting that the freight environment remained relatively strong despite the recent selloff.
"[But the] Middle East is pushing more oil to end users. Brazil and WAF barrels are heading to Europe as the arbitrage to the East is shut," the second trader continued.
Rising Middle Eastern inflows into Europe have also dampened demand in the sour crude segment with Johan Sverdrup differentials heard under further pressure Oct. 19.
"People have booked a lot of alternatives when Johan Svedrup was expensive," a Europe-based sour crude trader said.
The Buzzard oil field accounted for 15% of North Sea Forties Blend crude output in the week ended Oct. 8, Forties pipeline operator Ineos said Oct. 19.
The sulfur content of Forties Blend fell to 0.48%, while its gravity rose to 41.758 API, according to a conversion table on the company's website.
-- Ernest Puey

Raises all Mexican crude K factors to USWC
India, Europe decline across most crude grades
Regional pricing adjustments vary by market
Mexico's state-owned oil company Pemex lowered the K factors, or constant terms, for all its crude deliveries to the US Gulf Coast and east Asia in November, while raising them for deliveries to the US West Coast, according to data released Oct. 17 by the company's trading arm PMI.
To the USGC, the Atlantic Coast and the Caribbean, the K factor of Maya crude was dropped by $2.70/barrel to minus $14.15/b, while that of Zapoteco crude was cut by 60 cents/b to minus $6.70/b. The K factor of Isthmus crude was cut by 60 cents/b to minus $2.95/b and that of Olmeca crude was lowered $1.30/b to minus $1.75/b, the PMI data showed.
To the Americas' Pacific Coast, the K factor of Zapoteco crude was raised $1.60/b to $5.35/b while that of Olmeca and Isthmus crudes were hiked $1.10/b and 75 cents/b to $1.15/b and 85 cents/b, respectively. The K factor of Maya crude to the US West Coast rose 25 cents/b to minus $6.45/b.
K factors to Europe, the Middle East and India varied across the four key grades. The K factor of Olmeca crude to Europe and the Middle East was raised $1.05/b to minus $4.65/b, while that to India saw a hike of 10 cents/b to minus $5.90/b. The K-factors

Petroecuador resumes crude shipments from Colombia after nine-month pause
SOTE, OSLA pipelines handle 11,900 b/d from Colombia Ecuador and Colombia renew energy cooperation SOTE operating at capacity, Petroecuador says

Petroecuador has begun pumping crude from neighboring Colombia nine months after a trade war suspended operations.
Colombia's Ecopetrol and GeoPark subsidiary Amerisur are sending a total 11,900 barrels/day through Petroecuador's 360,000 b/d SOTE and 26-kilometer Lago Agrio-San Miguel, or OSLA, pipelines in a deal worth $16.8 million/year, Ecuador's state-run oil company said in a statement over the weekend.
Pumping operations restarted after investor-friendly Presidents Daniel Noboa of Ecuador and Abelardo de la Espriella of Colombia renewed energy cooperation agreements earlier in October.
De la Espriella's predecessor Gustavo Petro suspended electricity sales to Ecuador in January after President

Noboa imposed trade tariffs and cut off access to Colombian crude.
De la Espriella took office in August, pledging to renew ties with Ecuador, Peru, Chile and Argentina, with whom relations had been strained since Marxist President Petro rose to power in 2022.
The SOTE pipeline, which links Petroecuador's Lago Agrio oilfields with its 110,000 b/d Esmeraldas oil refinery on the northwestern Pacific coast, had been transporting oil from Colombia since 2013.
The pipeline is operating at capacity after repairing a leak and containing an oil spill in the week prior, the company added.
Ecuador was producing 466,080 b/d through Oct. 18, according to the Energy Ministry. Petroecuador accounts for about 80% of the country's output.
-- Alex Emery

Platts Asia and Middle Eastern Gasoline Daily Market Analysis
Malaysia's 95 RON gasoline exports rise
Asia 95/92 Inter-ron spread narrows to 10-month low
The Asian gasoline complex was expected to be rangebound to softer on Oct. 19 amid rising exports from Malaysia and as market participants continued to monitor Chinese gasoline exports.
One Singapore-based source said a surge in 95 RON exports from Malaysia had bolstered 95 RON gasoline supplies. Additionally, Petco sold 200,000 barrels of 95 RON physical gasoline during the Platts Market on Close assessment process in Singapore on Oct. 16.
Possibly reflecting a rise in regional 95 RON gasoline supplies, Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the FOB Singapore 95-92 inter-RON spread at a 10-month low of 35 cents/barrel on Oct. 16, narrowing from $1.65/b the previous day. The spread was previously narrower on Dec. 16, 2025, at 26 cents/b.
Brokers pegged the front-month FOB Singapore 92 RON gasoline swaps crack spread with Brent swaps in the $18.95$19/b range at 0300 GMT, down on day from the Platts assessment of $19.15/b at the Oct. 16 Asian close, Platts data showed.
Broking sources also placed the physical FOB Singapore 92 RON gasoline crack against front-month ICE Brent crude oil futures in the $23.40-$23.45/b range at 0300 GMT, down from the Platts assessment of $24.02/b at the Oct. 16 Asian close, the data showed.
In China, crude throughput recovered to a six-month high of 14.04 million barrels/day (57.48 million metric tons) in September, Platts previously reported.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Joshua Ong

Vietnam BSR pursues SAF, renewable diesel plant output by 2030
BSR plans 100,000-150,000 mt/year SAF plant
Refiner explores renewable diesel co-processing
Company secures crude supply through end-2026
Vietnam's state-owned refiner, Petrovietnam Refining and Petrochemical Corporation, is working to finalize an investment plan for a dedicated sustainable aviation fuel plant with a capacity of 100,000-150,000 metric tons/year, targeting production by 2030, while also exploring renewable diesel coprocessing at its existing Dung Quat refinery.
The push, disclosed at an investor workshop in Ho Chi Minh City, includes a dedicated domestic SAF production facility in Dung Quat, according to multiple local media reports on Oct. 16.
BSR has already produced commercial-scale trial batches of SAF and obtained ISCC EU certification.
The company also achieved ISCC PLUS certification for its sustainable marine fuel oil and launched B5 biodiesel.
The company is researching the feasibility of producing renewable diesel through co-processing technology, which would allow it to expand its green fuel portfolio using existing refinery infrastructure without a greenfield investment, according to reports.
On feedstock strategy, BSR said it has secured crude oil supply through end-2026 and is shifting its feedstock mix toward domestic sources in response to geopolitical risks in the Middle East and disruptions to international shipping routes.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed Sustainable Aviation Fuel HEFA-SPK FOB Straits, reflecting CORSIA-certified cargoes, at $2,180/metric ton Oct. 19, unchanged from Oct. 16.

CHINA DATA: Sept gasoil exports reach 43-month high of 1.85 mil mt
Export margins drive gasoil shipment surge
Jet fuel volumes drop 23.9% in one month
Fuel oil exports rise 114% MOM to 2.59 mil mt
China's gasoil exports reached 1.85 million metric tons (459,000 b/d) in September, a 43-month high, data from the General Administration of Customs showed Oct. 18.
The previous high was 2.15 million mt in February 2023, according to GAC data.
Analysts, refiners, and traders said the surge in gasoil exports, which began in July from about 430,000 mt in June, resulted from tight supplies in the global market and pushed up export margins.
The country's gasoil export margin rose $2.86/barrel month over month to $44.62/b by late September, while gasoil export margins improved $1.19/b to $8.33/b, according to S&P Global Energy CERA's monthly report dated Sept. 30.

Platts on Oct. 19 assessed the benchmark Gulf Coast Jet differential 15.70 cents/gallon stronger to an 11.50 cents/gal discount to NYMEX December ULSD futures, while the outright price rose 15.98 cents/gal to $4.611/gal.
Similar gains were observed on the Atlantic Coast, where the benchmark Buckeye Pipeline differential rose 12 cents/gal to an 8-cent/gal premium to NYMEX November ULSD futures, entering positive territory for the first time in 10 days.
Other US regions were quiet, but there was increased interest

Chinese oil companies usually prioritize jet fuel exports among the clean products amid limited export quotas to maximize export profits.
Due to the sharp increase in gasoil exports, a 262.3% yearover-year jump and a 31.6% month-over-month rise, China's conventional jet fuel and sustainable aviation fuel exports declined to 1.94 million mt in September from 2.55 million mt in August, GAC data showed.
As a result, clean oil product exports declined by 3.7% month over month to 4.41 million mt, GAC data showed.
Chinese refineries have resumed clean oil product exports after a temporary suspension during the National Day holiday over Oct. 1-7 and have been granted 4.54 million mt of new export quotas.
Meanwhile, they were awarded 1.07 million mt of fuel oil export quotas.
Looking forward, "Clean oil product exports are unlikely to see a further significant increase, unless crude imports recover to cross over 10 million b/d levels," said Minmin Hu, senior principal analyst with CERA.
Market sources estimated China's clean oil product exports at 2 million-3.85 million mt in October.
Fuel oil exports rose 114% from August to 2.59 million mt in September, rising 16.1% year over year, GAC data showed.
Strong fuel oil exports pushed China's refined oil product outflow to a 45-month high of 7.16 million mt in September, according to GAC.

US distillate stocks fall to near 22-week lows: survey
Diesel exports surge to potential record high Gasoline inventories rise, narrowing deficit

US distillate fuel oil stocks likely fell to nearly 22-week lows during the week to Oct. 16, analysts surveyed by Platts said Oct. 19, as the onset of planned and unplanned refinery maintenance began to weigh on runs.
Nationwide distillate stocks likely fell 700,000 barrels to about 101.9 million barrels during the period, analysts said. The fifth straight weekly draw would put stockpiles 14.6% behind

the seasonal five-year average of US Energy Information Administration data and leave them at the lowest outright level since late May.
US diesel and gasoil exports have averaged 1.7 million barrels/ day to date in October, data from S&P Global Commodities at Sea showed, up from 1.3 million b/d during September. At that rate, October 2026 exports would be in the running for an all-time high.
The arbitrage incentive for shipping ULSD from the US Gulf Coast to Northwest Europe has fallen to negative $1.13/barrel in October from $5.29/b during September, S&P Global Energy CERA data showed.
US gasoline stocks likely climbed 450,000 barrels to 208.7 million barrels, analysts said, narrowing the deficit to the seasonal five-year average to 5.4% from 5.9% the week prior.
Nationwide refinery utilization was likely unchanged at 93.5%, still 4.4 percentage points above the seasonal norm, although regional outages and the onset of fall maintenance may have reduced distillate output.
US refinery margins remain strong, though they have eased up into October. The USGC WTI MEH cracking margin averaged $44.93/b during the week to Oct. 15, CERA data showed, down from $50.13/b in the full month of September.
ULSD cracks have cooled from record-high levels seen last month. The USGC ULSD crack versus WTI MEH eased to $97.74/b last week from a September average of $101.03/b.
Gasoline and jet fuel cracks, CERA data showed, have fallen to $35.17/b and $82.72/b during the week to Oct. 15, respectively, from a September average of $53.97/b and $86.77/b, respectively, last month.
US crude stocks likely fell 3.57 million barrels to 415 million barrels, analysts said, putting inventories 3.5% behind the fiveyear average.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Binish Azhar
India cuts windfall tax on diesel, hikes gasoline, jet fuel unchanged
Government nearly halves diesel tax to 8.5 rupees/liter
Gasoline levy rises to 2.5 rupees/liter
India has cut the windfall gains tax on diesel exports but raised the tax on gasoline exports, and retained the tax for jet fuel at the same level, the country's oil ministry said in a statement released Oct. 16, reflecting tax adjustments for fuel exporters to ensure sufficient domestic fuel supplies amid ongoing global geopolitical tensions.
The new rates will be applicable for two weeks from Oct. 16, according to the officials.
The new rate of the windfall tax, also known as special additional excise duty (SAED), along with road and infrastructure cess on the export of diesel, will now be 8.5 Indian rupees/ liter, nearly halving from the earlier rate of 16 rupees/liter, the officials said.

© 2026 by S&P Global Inc. All rights reserved.

9

©2026 S&P Global Inc. Any unauthorized use of this copyrighted material constitutes infringement of our intellectual property and will subject you to possible legal action.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 19, 2026

The tax on overseas sale of petrol or gasoline was raised to 2.5 rupees/liter for the two weeks until Oct. 31, rising from the prior 0.5 rupees/liter, they added.
The tax on jet fuel was retained at 10.5 rupees/liter for the current fortnight, they said.
Market participants expect the reduction in the windfall tax for gasoil to have a limited impact on export volumes.
"There may be a few more cargoes [of gasoil], but not a major impact," an Asia-based middle distillates trader said.
"It's the first time in a while that the diesel export tax is less than that of jet fuel, so it could possibly result in some yield switch [by Indian refiners], exporting gasoil at the expense of jet fuel," a second trader said, adding that overall middle distillate export volumes will likely remain unimpacted.
However, refiners were unlikely to make the switch amid a widening regrade spread. The Platts-assessed front-month November swaps regrade -- the premium jet fuel commands over 10 ppm sulfur gasoil -- had averaged at plus $1.91/b over Oct. 1-16, flipping into positive territory from the September average of minus $3.09/b.
India introduced an export tax or duty on diesel and jet fuel on March 27 amid the Middle East conflict, and has been revising the tax rates every two weeks based on global crude and product prices, the ministry said.
India, the world's third-largest crude consumer and importer, sources 88% of its domestic demand through imports, according to the oil ministry.
The windfall tax has been levied to ensure domestic oil supply security by disincentivizing overseas sales, and aims to prevent oil product exporters from taking undue advantage of high global crude prices caused by geopolitical tensions such as RussiaUkraine and the Middle East conflicts, the ministry said.
-- Mei huey Ng, Rachelle Teo, Ratnajyoti Dutta
Diesel market remains under pressure as jet fuel crack widens in Europe
Jet fuel crack surpasses diesel in Europe
Stock release unlikely to significantly ease tightness
IEA plans 20 million barrel release soon
Europe's diesel market remains extremely tight despite jet fuel cracks outstripping gains in the motor fuel market for the first time in months, signaling a new and potentially more complex phase for European fuel markets.
After hovering around $60/barrel on average between Oct. 1 and Oct. 12, the European benchmark physical FOB ARA 10ppm diesel barge crack rose above $74/barrel on Oct. 16, for the first time since Sept. 30.
And while the European physical diesel crack stayed above the jet fuel crack since mid-June until mid-September, it now trades well below its counterpart.
The spread between the European jet fuel FOB FARAG bargeto-Brent crude crack and the ULSD FOB barge-to-Brent crude

crack was as wide as $11.75/b on Oct.16. "Everyone in Europe is so focused on diesel, even anticipating
a potential diesel crisis, and as a result refineries have not produced enough jet fuel," said James Simpson, head of aviation research at S&P Global Energy CERA. He added that in North America, it is a "very different story," with diesel prices trading around 5%-8% above jet fuel in recent weeks.
While the European benchmark diesel crack has rebounded in recent days since the beginning of October, it is still well below the peak of $98.01/b reached on Sept. 1.
But a higher jet fuel crack does not automatically mean that all refineries will maximize this fuel, with most of them potentially staying cautious, according to market participants.
"Gasoil cracks have pulled backed but are still very elevated, people continue to max out runs on diesel," a market source told Platts, part of S&P Global Energy.
The International Energy Agency said Oct.15 that it has specific plans in place to release more than 20 million barrels of diesel "as soon as possible." No details on the dates for release were provided. Only 8 million barrels of emergency oil stocks were released in September, marking a significant decline in inventories releases in recent months, according to IEA.
"Weaker [diesel] margins come from reinforcement on the crude side. With strong structure on the inputs, [we] automatically see weaker cracks. But nothing has changed on the products side," a European diesel producer told Platts.
New stock release announcements are not something which can support fuel market significantly, according to another market source. "Europe is releasing from an already announced quota and presenting that as news," the source said.
"Even if headlines are dictating price now, the structural and logistical problems should maintain tightness," according to the source, who asked for anonymity because of no authorization to speak publicly.
No lasting price relief in sight The diesel market situation is influenced by several factors,
including efforts to influence prices from the US, according to William O'Neil, principal analyst at S&P Global Energy CERA.
"On the federal level, this includes the dyed diesel waiver, supporting the G7 diesel inventory release, and the Russia diesel deal. Additionally, many US states have cut diesel taxes and loosened dyed diesel restrictions, echoing the federal waiver," O'Neil said.
S&P Global Energy assessed that half of US states have partially or fully waived dyed diesel rules, as of Oct. 16. The more important factor for the global diesel market is the perception that a US diesel export ban is becoming increasingly unlikely, according to O'Neil.
"But the levers of softening (with the exception of tax cuts in the US) probably aren't significant enough to deliver lasting price relief. We don't think the Russia deal, for example, is likely to substantially and durably lower global diesel prices unless Ukraine is on board," he said.

It is still unclear how much extra diesel supply Russia can add to the market in the coming months. Russian Deputy Prime Minister Alexander Novak said Oct. 9 that Russia is ready to export 300,000 metric tons of diesel in October, 500,000 mt in November and 1 million mt in December. He then said that Russian diesel deliveries can reach as much as 3.5 million mt/ month by the end of 2026, without clarifying the discrepancy between these figures.
Donald Trump has been pressuring Ukraine's President Volodymyr Zelenskyy to stop the country's attacks on Russian refineries. Zelenskyy said Oct. 15 that Ukrainian military officials were told by US counterparts that if they did not stop the energy strikes, the US would block intelligence sharing with Ukraine. But Ukraine is only ready to stop its attacks on Russian refineries if Russia stops attacking its energy facilities and killing Ukrainians, Zelenskyy said.
"We assess that Ukraine and Russia remain unlikely to adhere to a US-proposed ceasefire agreement that includes halting strikes on Russia's oil refineries," said Sascha Ostanina, Europe/CIS Country Risk principal analyst at S&P Global Market Intelligence.
"Previous US attempts to leverage limited aid to Ukraine-- including the suspension of intelligence sharing--as a means to compel Ukraine to halt strikes on Russia have not proven largely effective, and US intelligence had not been used in Russian refinery strikes either," Ostanina said, adding that Ukrainian authorities may anticipate a decrease in pressure from the US administration following the US midterm elections.
The 2026 US midterm elections is scheduled to take place on Nov. 3.
Around 45% of Russian primary refining capacity was offline as of the end of September, according to CERA.

Marine Fuel
Open European VLSFO arbitrage persists despite high dirty tanker freight costs
Smaller vessels transport fuel amid high rates
European fuel oil crack spread widens in October
An open arbitrage window for very low sulfur fuel oil and low sulfur fuel oil material from Europe to Singapore has remained open in recent days, prompting participants to move product despite the acutely elevated dirty tanker freight rates.
Higher premiums for LSFO in Singapore have persisted for much of October, making it economical to send product on the arbitrage, despite challenging freight costs.
The M1 FOB Singapore VLSFO cargo premium over Balmo FOB Rdam VLSFO barges last stood at $131.25/metric ton, Oct. 16, according to assessments by Platts, part of S&P Global Energy. This marks a major increase from a July low of $15.25/mt.
Market participants note that smaller vessel sizes are being

used to transport fuel oil on this arbitrage window, as they have slightly more favorable freight costs compared to the acute Suezmax rates.
"There are still Suezmaxes, but there were also smaller sizes in the past weeks... Afra, Panamax and even MR," said an Europefocused trader source.
"There has been some volume fixed, to both Singapore and US Atlantic Coast, mostly on MRs and Panamaxes," a second source said.
Open arbitrage has provided an alternative outlet for VLSFO demand, a dynamic reflected in the paper market, where the front-month VLSFO barge crack against Brent has trended higher through October.
Platts assessed the front-month 0.5% sulfur fuel oil barge crack spread -- measuring the relative price of the product relative to the crude from which it is made -- at $2.166/barrel on Oct. 16, 61 cents/b wider day over day and also the highest level since Sept. 11, when the spread was assessed at $2.31/b, reflecting a one-month high.
-- Tommy Petrou, Joseph Jaffe

FUJAIRAH DATA: Bunker fuel sales hold above 200,000 cubic meters in September for third month
Sales climb 4.8% MOM in September Volume drops 63% from September 2025 Low sulfur fuel oil's share falls to 60.49%

Ship fuel sales at the UAE's Port of Fujairah climbed 4.8% in September from August, holding above 200,000 cubic meters for the third consecutive month after a record low in June, according to port data as of Oct. 18.
The recorded volume of 238,982 cubic meters in September was still down 63% year over year, according to the data. Bunker fuel sales have fallen as shipping disruptions through the Strait of Hormuz curbed demand, with the record low at 88,659 cu m in June, according to port data.
Since then, ship fuel sales have been above 200,000 cu m. Marine gasoil sales more than tripled from August and were five times higher than in September 2025, according to the data.

Feedstocks
Platts European Naphtha Daily Market Analysis
Tight supply supports European prices
Gasoline-naphtha spreads declines significantly
Freight costs surge amid Middle East attacks
European naphtha remained supported by constrained supply availability Oct. 19, while gasoline-naphtha spreads fell significantly.
The Platts Market on Close assessment process was active, with five outstanding bids and two outstanding offers.
Heavy virgin naphtha values were under pressure amid weaker reforming economics, reducing refiners' incentive to process the grade into higher-octane reformate.
"Heavy virgin naphtha is a bit off, given new reforming margins," a player active in the naphtha market said, "Octanes are no longer strong."
Gasoline arbitrage economics out of Northwest Europe are currently unfavorable, keeping product within the region and weighing down blendstock demand.
"[Blending demand is] slower now, not many gasoline outlets workable for balance month," the player said.
However, the petrochemical sector has been providing a floor to prices. Chemical players have stepped back into the buy-side amid rising propane prices.
The propane-naphtha spread has been narrowing significantly, which means that propane's competitive advantage at the cracker feedslate has fallen.
On the global scale, clean freight remains extremely elevated, with four ship attacks reported over Oct. 16-18, including a rocket-propelled grenade attack on a tanker near Jubail on the east coast of Saudi Arabia, according to UKMTO Operations Centre.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the physical naphtha CIF NWE cargo price at $928.50/metric ton, up $7.50/mt.
Platts assessed the front-month CIF NWE naphtha swap up $10.50/mt at $893/mt, while the crack was down 65 cents/b at minus $4.60/b.
Platts assessed the October/November time spread steady at $29.50/mt, while the November/December spread was up 25 cents/mt at $31.50/mt.
The front-month East-West spread -- the spread between the CFR Japan naphtha cargo swap and the CIF NWE equivalent -- was at $74.50/mt, down $6.50/mt.
-- Barbara Fernandez-pita

Gas Liquids
Platts Asian LPG Daily Commentary
North Asia LPG prices climb on crude gains
Butane-propane spread widens to minus $80/mt
Asian propane-naphtha swaps spread narrows
CFR North Asia LPG prices rose at the start of the Asian trading week on Oct. 19, supported by a $23.12/metric ton increase in front-month Brent crude futures amid escalating unrest in the Middle East.
Four ship attacks were reported over Oct. 16-18, including a rocket-propelled grenade attack on a tanker near Jubail on the east coast of Saudi Arabia, according to UK Maritime Trade Operations, Platts reported earlier.
The spread between CFR North Asia propane and butane cargoes widened to minus $80/mt from minus $75/mt in the previous session.
Two Asia-based market sources attributed the widening spread to stronger seasonal demand for butane as a blending component in winter-grade gasoline in the West. Meanwhile, the ongoing conflict in the Middle East continues to constrain butane supplies from the Persian Gulf region, the sources added.
Platts assessed CFR North Asia propane and butane cargoes at $977.50/metric ton and $1,057.50/mt, respectively, on Oct. 19, up $12.50/mt and $17.50/mt from the previous session.
Platts assessed CFR East China propane at $976/mt on Oct. 19, up $12.50/mt from the previous session.
Platts assessed CFR South China propane at $972.50/mt on Oct. 19, up $12.50/mt day over day.
A bid and an offer were heard during the trading session on Oct. 19, but no trades were concluded at the market close.
Platts assessed the CFR North Asia propane-to-C&F Japan naphtha front-month swap spread at minus $13.25/mt on Oct. 19, narrowing from minus $15.75/mt in the previous session.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Jeff Ong

Platts assessed freight on the 260,000 metric ton West AfricaEast route at w700 Oct. 19, down w25 day over day.

Sentiment in the West Africa Suezmax market weakened as "owners aren't seeing much to offer into," a broker said.

Platts Americas Dirty Tanker Daily Commentary
VLCC Brazil-China route tests w70 lower
Suezmax rates see downward correction
Aframax Vancouver-China run falls $1 million on day
Freight for the Americas VLCC and Suezmax segments fell on Oct. 19, following reports that limited activity continued to weigh on rates and that position lists were lengthening.
For the VLCCs, a downward correction was seen out of Brazil, with the latest deal one in which Petrobras booked the Delta Amazon for a Nov. 18-19 loading Brazil-East run at w615, with demurrage set at $615,000/day. Discharge options to East Coast India and West Coast India were also reported at w650 and w665, respectively.
Prior to covering, there were reports of about 6-8 offers on the cargo, indicating the availability of tonnage following a sustained period of limited fixing activity.
This has led to increased "competition among owners for the limited number of visible cargoes," a shipbroker said.

Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the 260,000 metric ton Brazil-China run down w70 to w615.
While the US Gulf Coast market remained untested, indications fell from the lower levels seen in Brazil.
Platts assessed the benchmark 270,000 mt USGC-China route sinking $4.5 million to $65 million, in line with the bulk of market indications.
Market participants also eyed midsize tanker rates, as "the softening trend across the smaller classes does nothing to bolster VLCC sentiment and instead reinforces the bearish tone prevailing throughout the Americas," the same broker added.
For the Suezmaxes, the lone fixture reported was a Nov. 19-20 loading of a Brazil-US West Coast run, with Petrobras booking the Noble. However, the rate could not be confirmed before the close of the Oct. 19 Platts Market on Close assessment process.
Calls for both the Suezmax Guyana-UK Continent and USGCUKC routes were heard softening amid the overall lack of activity.
A second shipbroker said that it was a "quiet correction" as activity was "very slow all over the place."
Platts assessed the benchmark 130,000 mt Guyana-UKC run down w25 to w875, and the 145,000 mt USGC-UKC run down w10 to w790.
Aframax rates steady to softer Aframax rates in the Atlantic Basin held mostly steady,
showing resilience despite a growing position list. One trans-Atlantic fixture was reported, with Occidental
booking the Tavistock Square at w650, with demurrage at $275,000/d, matching the previously reported level. Two market participants expressed surprise that the deal was not lower, given the available tonnage. Nevertheless, active market indications were unanimous at w650.
Platts assessed the benchmark 70,000 mt USGC-UKC route unchanged at w650.
"I think more corrections down are coming," a third shipbroker said. "But we can't be too far from the bottom, which should spur some activity."
A fourth shipbroker echoed that sentiment, and a fifth said that "all owners would agree" that w650 was the floor.
In Vancouver, a "two-tier market [is] emerging," a sixth shipbroker said, as booking interest from the Far East outpaced that for runs to the US West Coast.
"I have five, six owners willing [to do a Far East run]," the sixth shipbroker said, "and none interested in local without [direct continuation to the Far East]."
Indeed, Platts reported two trans-Pacific fixtures on Oct. 19, with Suncor's booking of the TP Intrepid considered the most recent. Market participants said the deal was done last week, with reported rates ranging from $14 million to $15 million; Platts could not confirm the rate before the MOC.
Platts assessed the benchmark 80,000 mt Vancouver-China run $1 million lower at $14.5 million, while the benchmark 80,000 mt Vancouver-USWC route was assessed unchanged at w900.
-- Catherine Rogers, Lucas Casas

News
Netbacks jump for West African refined products as freight costs bite
Spreads between Offshore, inland jump in WAF
West African clean freight costs surge to 7-month highs
West African refined products are seeing wide spreads between offshore markets and discharge ports, as high freight costs for cross-WAF voyages widen netbacks for traders in the region.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the clean West Africa-West Africa 37kt rate at $19.73/metric ton on Oct. 19, which was the highest level since April 15.
Gasoil delivered into Ghana, for example, was heard offered as high as $130/mt against front-month ICE LSGO futures. In the Offshore Lome market, meanwhile, product was heard trading closer to $50/mt against the same contract.
High freight costs are also widening the spreads between Offshore Lome and Nigerian depots.
Platts assessed the Low-Sulfur Diesel STS Lome price at a $70/mt premium to ICE LSGO futures on Oct. 19, with a flat price of $1,520.25/mt. Meanwhile, depot prices in Lagos were heard on offer as high as 1,850 naira/liter, or approximately $1,641.75/mt.
Traders said the market was placing large premiums on delivered product amid tight West of Suez freight rates.
Freight costs from Europe into West Africa are also high, keeping locational spreads wide.
Platts assessed the FOB NWE WAF-spec gasoline at $1,108.25/ mt Oct. 19, compared with $1,188.25/mt for STS Lome, on the back of UKC-WAF MR freight rates, which are persistently above $50/mt.
Expensive freight was "keeping the [WAF] arb shut" for gasoline traders in Europe, according to a trader.
"There is some buying demand [in Nigeria]," said a second gasoline trader. "But levels are maybe not too attractive."
-- Matthew Tracey-cook
Trump waives Russia oil sanctions to preserve Ukraine peace talks
India, China are top importers of Russian oil
Lawmakers from both parties oppose the waiver

Trump also said that Russia is doing little, if anything, to help Iran, and he wants to keep it that way, according to the post.
"Lastly, Zelenskyy is using Diesel Refineries in Russia as a target, knowing that these attacks are seriously hurting the world diesel markets, including those in the United States," Trump said.
Trump, on Sept. 18 signed comprehensive sanctions legislation targeting major purchasers of Russian oil and gas. The law gave Trump until Oct. 18 to impose the new sanctions, but also allowed the president to waive them.
The legislation allows the president to impose tariffs of up to 100% on goods imported from the five largest buyers of Russian crude oil or natural gas by total volume over the year before enactment, among other measures.
Executive order Late Oct. 16, the White House issued an executive order
waiving all sanctions and duties under the law, saying it is in the US national interest to do so. The waivers will remain until the president or the Secretary of State, in consultation with the Secretary of the Treasury and the Trade Representative, determines further action is needed.
While the White House has issued waivers for now, it may be keeping its options open for the future, ClearView Energy Partners said in an Oct. 17 note. The executive order sets the tariff on goods from the top five buyers of Russian oil and gas at 100%, the maximum under the law, if countries make new purchases of Russian oil or gas on or after Oct. 18, 2026, the note said.
"This comes as little surprise given the president's historical predilection for statutory tariff tools," the ClearView note said. The order also created a monitoring protocol to determine whether further action is needed, including regarding the waivers, the note said.
Analysts had expected that Trump would waive the sanctions and tariffs, saying that imposing new restrictions on Russian fossil fuels would contradict Trump's waiver allowing sales of Russian diesel fuel to the global market.

US President Donald Trump has indefinitely waived new legally mandated sanctions and tariffs on the largest buyers of Russian oil, saying he does not want to endanger talks to end the war with Ukraine.
"My decision on Russia is a very simple one. I am in the process of negotiating peace in the war between Russia and Ukraine, and don't want to do anything that will drive, foolishly, Russia away from the bargaining table," Trump said on his Truth Social media site Oct. 18.

The five largest importers of Russian seaborne crude for the 12 months ending in August 2026 were India, China, Italy, Turkey and the Netherlands, according to data from S&P Global Commodities at Sea.
India imported an average of 1.8 million b/d, China imported

Congressional opposition Lawmakers who supported passage of the law have voiced
strong opposition to the waiver. Senator Ted Cruz (Republican-Texas) said the waiver benefits
Putin, which is not a good idea. "I wish the president hadn't done it. I think it was a mistake," Cruz said Oct.17 on Fox News.
Senator Richard Blumenthal of Connecticut, a lead Democratic sponsor of the legislation, also slammed the decision. "These waivers have nothing to do with our national security and everything to do with sucking up to Vladimir Putin," Blumenthal said Oct. 16 on X, formerly known as Twitter.
Representative Gregory Meeks (Democrat-New York) on Oct. 16 introduced legislation to block Trump's Russian diesel waiver, tighten sanctions on Russian energy and prohibit future licenses for Russian oil, petroleum products, diesel and natural gas purchase, sale, delivery, or offloading.
The measure would also prevent the US International Development Finance Corporation from supporting any energy deal owned by a Russian national, regardless of whether the assets are in Russia.
DFC is prohibited from supporting a project in a designated country of concern, including Russia.

Hormuz traffic climbs to 23 ships on more products, bulk transits
Product tanker crossings rise to five ships
Refined exports increase to 1.1M barrels
Weekly Hormuz transits drop to 154 ships

Trafigura went into the Persian Gulf on Oct. 18, marking its first arrival in the region since November 2025 after repositioning from the Americas, according to CAS data.
Six VLCCs The six VLCCs that transited Hormuz on Oct. 17 all exited the
Gulf: the Liberia-flagged Arzanah carrying Basrah Heavy crude from Iraq, the Cameroon-flagged Matelo, the Saudi Arabiaflagged Samha, the Kuwait-flagged Umm al Aish, the Liberiaflagged Emerald Shine and the Panama-flagged Kiku operated by Trafigura, according to CAS data. Kiku left the Gulf around Oct. 17 in laden condition, although the cargo is not known, CAS said.
Trafigura said it had no comment on its ships. South Korea's Sinokor-operated VLCC Peru reappeared off India's west coast on Oct. 18 after leaving Hormuz around Oct. 16, laden with Saudi Arabia's Arab Medium grade crude and was last seen heading for Vadinar, India, where it is expected to conduct ship-to-ship transfers, CAS said. Sinokor's Egypt Prosperity exited Hormuz on Oct. 16 in laden condition and was last seen heading toward offshore Fujairah, UAE, CAS said. The Panama-flagged LPG carrier Yellow Stone left the Gulf on Oct. 17 after loading at Sitra, Bahrain, in August, CAS said. The ship had spent nearly two months between Port Rashid in Dubai and Mina Saqr in Ras al Khaimah, both in the UAE, CAS said. For the week ending Oct. 18, total Strait of Hormuz traffic was estimated at 154 transits, down from 160 transits in the week prior, CAS said. Over the same period, crossings through the US naval blockade line dropped to 430 transits from 443 transits, while Bab al-Mandab traffic dropped to 266 ships from 279, according to CAS data.

Strait of Hormuz transits climbed to 23 ships on Oct. 18, with more product tankers and bulk carriers taking the chokepoint into and out of the Gulf, S&P Global Commodities at Sea said in an Oct. 19 report.
Product tanker transits rose to five from four ships, while bulk carriers had eight crossings on Oct. 18, up from zero on Oct. 17, CAS said. Total Hormuz transits rose from 15 ships on Oct. 17, according to the CAS report.
In line with the increased product traffic, refined product exports through Hormuz rose to 1.1 million barrels on Oct. 18 from 995,335 barrels on Oct. 17, according to CAS figures.
Crude exports fell to 7.35 million barrels from 7.94 million barrels over the same period, according to CAS.
VLCC traffic dropped to just one ship on Oct. 18 from six on Oct. 17, according to the CAS report. Transits through the Bab al-Mandab Strait into the Red Sea dropped to 34 ships on Oct. 18 from 39 on Oct. 17, according to CAS data.

Refinery updates
Dangote targets January 2030 launch for Kenya project
Refinery: Lamu, Kenya
Owner: Dangote Group, regional governments
Overall capacity: 700,000 b/d
The founder of Nigeria's Dangote Group has committed to launching the company's second 700,000 b/d refinery in Lamu, Kenya, by January 2030, he announced at the site's groundbreaking ceremony last month, as regional governments have lined up for stakes in the new project.
In an official launch ceremony attended by Kenyan president William Ruto and his Ugandan counterpart, Yoweri Museveni,

the Nigerian industrialist announced his new 700,000-barrel/day refinery project will involve building 1000 MW of power capacity, double the scale of his Lagos facility, as well as a one-millionmetric-ton polypropylene complex and a base oils unit.
Rwanda has approached the Dangote Group over the possibility of owning a stake in its upcoming oil refinery in Kenya, its president, Paul Kagame, said on state-owned radio Oct. 18, making it the latest African government to express interest in the development, alongside Uganda, Kenya and Tanzania.
The company has set a maximum 30% threshold it is willing to allocate to government stakeholders in the project, after previous engagement with Kenya, Uganda, Tanzania and Ethiopia, a Dangote refinery executive told Platts, part of S&P Global Energy.
As part of its plans to fast-track the refinery construction, Dangote has already awarded a $450 million contract to Engineers India Limited, a previous partner on its Nigerian site, to manage and provide consultancy services on the project.
Sitting alongside some of the world's oldest and bestpreserved Swahili settlements, the Lamu project has drawn backlash from local residents and environmentalists, who have rallied to block the refinery's construction. A case raised by 133 local residents against the refinery did not result in an order to suspend construction on Oct. 14 due to errors with the application, although the complainants were granted another week to correct their filing.
-- Kelly Norways

REFINERY NEWS: Australia's Lytton to complete FCC works in mid-November
Refinery: Lytton, Australia
Owner: Ampol
Overall capacity: 109,000 barrels/day
Australia's Ampol said in a trading update Oct. 18 that the FCC at its Lytton refinery is expected to restart from maintenance in mid-November.
It also said that during the "detailed inspection" of the FCC, "management identified additional reactor refractory relining work and an opportunity to bring forward maintenance activities on one of the refinery's crude units."
The company added that completing the works under the current turnaround "is considered the most economically efficient approach, captures efficiencies from labor already mobilized and supports reliable production and fuel availability through 2027."
It added that customer supply is not expected to be affected. Ampol previously said that the FCC work started on July 30 and start-up was expected in October. As a result of the delayed restart, it expects production of high-value products to be approximately 150 million liters lower in Q4. Ampol also said Oct. 18 that Lytton increased production to 1.137 billion liters in Q3, up from 1.252 billion liters in the yearearlier period and to 4.081 billion liters in January-September, up from 3.961 billion liters.

REFINERY NEWS: Phillips 66 Sweeny refinery reports coker unit upset
Refinery: Sweeny, Texas
Owner: Phillips 66
Overall capacity: 277,000 b/d
Units affected: Coker
Duration: Oct. 16-17
Phillips 66 reported an emissions event at its Sweeny Refinery after a process upset at Unit 26.2 led to flaring from the refinery's coker flare system, according to an Oct. 17 filing with local regulators.
According to a filing submitted to the Texas Commission on Environmental Quality, the upset began at 11:50 p.m. CDT on Oct. 16 and was resolved by 3:51 a.m. Oct. 17. Operations personnel worked to stabilize the unit and return it to normal operating conditions.
The event resulted in the release of an estimated 16,129 lb of sulfur dioxide, 624 lb of carbon monoxide, 175 lb of hydrogen sulfide and 122 lb of nitrogen oxides, along with smaller quantities of volatile organic compounds and hydrocarbons. Emissions were routed through the coker flare.
Phillips 66 said the upset occurred in Unit 26.2 -- which is a coker -- but did not specify the cause of the operational disruption beyond describing it as a process upset that resulted in flaring. Emissions estimates were based on continuous emissions monitoring system data and engineering calculations.

Subscriber Notes
Platts to amend freight calculation for Oman, Murban CFR North Asia crude assessments Nov. 2
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, will amend the freight calculation used in its daily Oman and Murban CFR North Asia crude assessments, effective Nov. 2, 2026.
Under the change, Platts will use the daily VLCC Gulf of Omanto-Far East freight assessment in $/mt (DOOEW00), which was launched Aug. 3, in its calculation of the outright price of Oman and Murban delivered into North Asia at 4:30 pm Singapore time (0830 GMT).
The change will better reflect the shipping economics for the two grades, with Murban primarily loading from Fujairah port and Oman from Mina Al Fahal port outside the Strait of Hormuz.
Currently, the freight calculation is based on the daily VLCC Persian Gulf-to-Far East freight assessment in $/mt (TDAAB00).
Platts first proposed the change in a subscriber note published Sept. 28, 2026.
The freight calculation will continue to use the conversion factor for metric tons to barrels, as applied to the grades, in line with their prevailing qualities. The conversion factors are currently 7.32 for Oman and 7.62 for Murban.
In line with this change, Platts will also use the daily VLCC Gulf of Oman-to-Far East worldscale freight assessment (DIOEW00) in the freight calculation for Murban in its Indonesian Minas, Cinta and Widuri crude market parity prices.
Under the current MPP methodology for Minas, Cinta and Widuri, Murban -- as one of five basket grades -- is normalized for delivery to Singapore, accounting for freight. This is currently calculated using the daily VLCC Persian Gulf-to-Singapore

Platts amends Abu Dhabi crude OSPs publication Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, has updated its publication
of Abu Dhabi crude official selling prices effective Oct. 8, 2026, in line with changes announced by Abu Dhabi National Oil Co. (ADNOC).
In July, ADNOC said it would adopt a prompt-month pricing methodology based on the Platts Dubai benchmark (PCAAT00) plus an ADNOC-announced differential for the OSPs of its Abu Dhabi onshore and offshore crude grades, with effect from November 2026 loadings. ADNOC added that it would announce the OSP differentials for Murban, Das, Umm Lulu and Upper Zakum in the month prior to the delivery month.
Accordingly, Platts will publish the monthly OSP differentials for the four grades, basis Platts Dubai, beginning with the November differentials issued by ADNOC on Oct. 8, 2026.
Platts first announced the change in a subscriber note published on Aug. 31, 2026.
The OSP differentials are published under the following reinstated codes:

Grade Murban Umm Lulu Das Blend Upper Zakum

OSP Diff to Dubai AAIOS00 OSLUD00 AAXPL00 AAIOV00

Platts previously published the OSP differentials under these codes until May 2021, when they were then suspended due to a change in the ADNOC pricing methodology.
From November, the existing codes for the outright OSPs for Murban (AAIKZ00), Upper Zakum (AAILC00), Das Blend (AAXPH00) and Umm Lulu (OSLUF00) will remain unchanged. Platts will publish the outright OSPs of the four grades following the publication of the average of Platts Dubai crude assessments

Platts to launch daily FOB Middle East bitumen assessments from Nov. 2
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, will launch daily VG30 and VG40 bitumen spot assessments for FOB Middle East, effective Nov. 2, 2026.
The new assessments will complement Platts' existing daily and weekly bitumen assessments, providing greater price transparency and information in the waterborne trade for the product in the Asia-Pacific and Middle East markets.
Platts will launch two new daily outright assessments and their respective monthly averages. These assessments will reflect bulk viscosity grade 30 and VG40 grade bitumen, as per IS 73:2013 specifications, loading 10-30 days forward from the date of publication in the Middle East.

Assessment FOB Middle East Bitumen VG30 FOB Middle East Bitumen VG40

Quantity

Laycan

2,500-10,000 mt, bulk 10-30 days forward

UoM $/mt

2,500-10,000 mt, bulk 10-30 days forward $/mt

Pricing information for material with other specifications, such as PEN 60/70, may be considered for the assessments, normalized to the proposed standard.
The assessments will reflect market value at the close of Asian trading at 4:30 pm Singapore time and follow the Singapore publication schedule. Bids, offers and trades reported throughout the day may be considered in the assessment process, with values normalized to the market close at 4:30 pm Singapore time.
This follows a proposal published Sept. 14 and available here. Please send any questions, comments or feedback to asia_products@spglobal.com, with a copy to pricegroup@spglobal.com. For written comments, please provide a clear indication if they are not intended for publication by Platts for public viewing. Platts proposes to amend rollover date of Asian Dated Brent Strip at London close
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, is proposing to amend the rollover date of its Asian Dated Brent Strip at London close assessment to the first publication day of the month, from Dec. 1, 2026.
The proposed change, which follows Platts review of historical data sets, would align the ADB Strip at London close assessment with the current rollover date of its ADB Strip at Asia close assessment.
Currently, this assessment, which reflects the forward value of

Dated Brent two months ahead of the date of publication at the 4:30 pm London close, rolls over on the 9th of the month.
The assessments affected by this change appear in Crude Oil Marketwire, Asia Pacific/Arab Gulf Marketscan, Oilgram Price Report, Platts Global Alert/ Platts Crude Oil Alert pages 2206, 2213, 2215, 2217, 2219, 2260, 2261, 2262, 2263, 2264, 2265, 2266, 2267, 2268, 2269, 2270, 2271, 2280, 2281, 2558, 2559, and under their price database codes as below:

Platts revises measurement units, descriptions for FO Licht ethanol prices
The following Platts FOLicht ethanol prices have had their units of measurement and descriptions updated, as summarized below.
Cubic Meter Pure Alcohol (CPA) · Type: Volume Unit · Definition: A unit of volume that measures the volume of pure (100%) alcohol. One CBMPA equals 1 cubic meter (1,000 liters) of pure alcohol. Unlike a standard cubic meter (m³), which measures the total volume of any substance (liquid, gas, or solid), CBMPA refers specifically to the ethanol content, not the total liquid volume.

Units of measurements and descriptions updated for Platts FOLicht ethanol prices The following Platts FOLicht ethanol prices have had their Units of measurements and descriptions updated, as summarized below. Hectoliter Pure Alcohol (HPA) · Type: Volume Unit · Definition: A unit of volume that measures the volume of pure (100%) alcohol. One HLTPA equals 1 hectoliter (100 liters) of pure alcohol. Unlike a standard hectoliter (hL), which measures the total volume of any liquid, HLTPA refers only to the pure alcohol content, regardless of the concentration in the original beverage.

Platts proposes to launch weighted indexes for Chicago CBOB, ULSD
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, is proposing to launch Chicago basket indexes for gasoline and diesel from March 2, incorporating the independent assessments for the major pipelines and Buckeye Complex and weighting them by trading volume, as well as adding a new, separate assessment for Chicago West Shore and Badger pipeline ULSD.
Feedback from market participants has indicated the need for Chicago-weighted basket indexes that reflect recent volumes traded on the Badger, West Shore, and Wolverine pipelines, as well as the Buckeye Complex.
The proposed weightings would be calculated by tracking recent trades across the West Shore, Badger, and Wolverine pipelines, as well as the Buckeye Complex. Total reported trades would be compiled on a cyclical basis, assuming a minimum volume of 10,000 barrels where not specified. These trades will then be sorted by pipeline to determine the percentage contribution of each component.
The weighted index would be calculated from the average of the differentials to NYMEX futures for the West Shore/Badger pipelines, Wolverine pipeline and BCX assessments, adjusted by

© 2026 by S&P Global Inc. All rights reserved.

28

©2026 S&P Global Inc. Any unauthorized use of this copyrighted material constitutes infringement of our intellectual property and will subject you to possible legal action.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 19, 2026

their corresponding weightings. For example, if Platts is assessing Cycle 2 for Chicago ULSD
and traded volumes from the previous cycles came out to 20% West Shore and Badger, 30% Wolverine and 50% Buckeye Complex and the pipeline differentials were assessed at plus 2 cents/gal, plus 3 cents/gal and plus 3.50 cents/gal, respectively, the index basket differential would be plus 3.05 cents/gal.
Platts' current assessments for Chicago CBOB and ULSD are as follows:

Symbol AATHA00 AGCWC00 ACBAE00 AAREL00 ACBAA00 AGCWA00

Name ULSD Chicago pipeline ULSD Chicago Wolverine Pipeline ULSD Chicago BCX Gasoline CBOB Chicago pipeline Gasoline CBOB Chicago BCX Gasoline CBOB Chicago Wolverine Pipeline

The proposed Chicago basket indexes would be as follows:
Name Gasoline CBOB Chicago Basket ULSD Chicago Basket
Platts is concurrently seeking feedback on how to incorporate West Shore and Badger into the weighted index when the 1 psi waiver is in place during the summer. More information on how Platts will adjust the West Shore and Badger assessments in accordance with the 1 psi waiver can be found here.
Additionally, Platts seeks feedback on the naming conventions for the proposal. Platts proposes to amend the assessment name from Gasoline CBOB Chicago pipeline to Gasoline CBOB West Shore and Badger pipeline to better represent the products assessed. This change follows the Feb. 3, 2025, removal of the Wolverine Pipeline from the basket underpinning the generic Chicago assessment due to the anticipated removal of the 1 psi waiver in multiple Midwest states and a division in RVP.
Platts is also proposing to launch a separate West Shore/ Badger assessment for Chicago ULSD; this would not replace the existing generic assessment, which would remain unchanged. The name of the new assessment would be ULSD Chicago West Shore and Badger Pipeline.
The indexes would appear on PGA pages 350, 351, 154, 24, 482, 410 and 411, as well as in US Marketscan, Oilgram Price Report, and on Platts Connect.

Please send all feedback and comments by Jan. 23 to americas_products@spglobal.com and pricegroup@spglobal.com.
For written comments, please provide a clear indication if comments are not intended for publication by Platts for public viewing. Platts will consider all comments received and will make comments not marked as confidential available upon request.
Platts invites feedback on US gasoline RVP calendar for 2026 As part of its commitment to open and transparent pricing
and product specifications, Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, would like to invite feedback regarding publishing dates and/or cycles for 2026 US gasoline RVP shifts.
The 2026 calendar largely follows 2025 norms, with no notable deviations from prior years. The calendar will be updated throughout the year as more information becomes available.
The shift dates are based on relevant pipeline schedules, historical information, and feedback from market participants.
US Gulf Coast shifts are based on the Colonial Pipeline schedule. US Atlantic Coast shifts are based on Buckeye Pipeline and Colonial Pipeline calendars.
On the West Coast, Los Angeles, San Francisco and Arizona RVP changes are based on Kinder Morgan's SFPP schedule, while Portland and Seattle shifts are based on Olympic Pipeline's calendar.
In the Midwest, Group 3 shifts are based on the ONEOK Pipeline schedule, while Chicago shifts are based on Explorer Pipeline and Buckeye Complex schedules.
The US Environmental Protection Agency removed a waiver in eight Midwestern states that allowed for an additional 1 psi during the summer. Platts will adjust the RVP Calendar for Chicago CBOB in accordance with the implementation of the waiver. More information can be found here.
For questions or comments, please contact americas_products@spglobal.com with a cc to pricegroup@spglobal.com.
For written comments, please provide a clear indication if comments are not intended for publication by Platts for public viewing. Platts will consider all comments received and will make comments not marked as confidential available upon request.
